#3092. Pfizer's Launched and Acquired Drugs Grew 32% in Q3 — and 3 Other Reasons to Buy the Stock Before 2027

Pfizer remains deeply unloved on Wall Street, but it is slowly turning a corner.

By Reuben Gregg Brewer – Nov 20, 2026 at 5:15PM EST

Pfizer (PFE -0.51%) faces a large patent cliff, and investors worry it won't be able to offset the revenue it loses as older drugs face generic competition. That's a problem, but the drugmaker is aware of it and is making important strides in its efforts to address the issue. The big story, however, is that this issue is pretty normal for a drug company.

In fact, revenue from recently launched and acquired drugs rose 32% in the third quarter of 2026. That shows the pharmaceutical giant's efforts are bearing fruit. But there's more reason to be excited, including the company's strong balance sheet, its large dividend, and management's commitment to continue paying that dividend. Here's what you need to know.

One of the biggest worries about Pfizer is its upcoming patent expirations. Two big ones are Eliquis in 2028 and Vyndamax in 2031. But patent expirations are a normal part of the pharma sector. And research and development efforts in the highly technical and intensely competitive healthcare sector don't always align perfectly with a company's patent expiration schedule. It's just a fact of life.

The 32% revenue growth Pfizer achieved from launched and acquired products is evidence that the company is seeing some success in its efforts to address the issue. And it sets up three more reasons to consider buying the stock.

Pfizer ended the third quarter of 2026 with $1.8 billion in cash and $12.4 billion in short-term investments. That gives the company significant firepower to support its business through this patent-expiration cycle. In fact, the company's cash hoard is slightly higher than at the start of the year, so it isn't rapidly burning through its savings.

Despite the company's results with new drugs and its financial strength, investors are deeply negative on Pfizer's stock. That's highlighted by its huge 6.1% dividend yield as of this writing. To put that yield into context, the S&P 500 index (^GSPC -3.70%) yields roughly 1.1%, and the average pharma stock yields 1.3%.

The negativity highlighted by the elevated yield is echoed by more traditional valuation metrics. Pfizer's price-to-sales and price-to-book ratios are below their five-year averages. And while the price-to-earnings ratio is elevated, it is largely due to one-time charges. Looking at the whole picture, Pfizer's stock looks attractively priced.

Given the lofty yield, Pfizer is likely to be most appealing to dividend-focused investors. But right now earnings aren't covering the dividend. That's not a great sign, but one-time charges are part of the issue. If you look at cash flow, the company can still support the dividend. That's highlighted by a cash dividend payout ratio of roughly 85%. While a lower number would be better, there's still some room for adversity. And don't forget about the company's large cash pile.

Both of these facts help support management's strong backing of the dividend. Indeed, one of Pfizer's key capital priorities is to "maintain and grow our dividend." Obviously, the company's priorities could change, but it wouldn't make a statement like that if the board of directors wasn't on board with protecting the dividend.

At the end of the day, Pfizer is facing very real headwinds today. But it is an industry-leading company with a long history of success. If history is any guide, it will muddle through this difficult period. The success it is having with new drugs, its financial strength, attractive valuation, and dedication to its large dividend are all strong reasons for investors to take a deep dive before 2026 is over. If you wait too long, the opportunity here may pass you by.

#3082. BigBear.ai vs. Innodata: Which Technology Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

One is built on federal and defense contracts, the other on training AI models for big tech, and their financial profiles diverge sharply.

By Sara Appino – Updated Nov 20, 2026 at 5:29PM EST

10 stocks we like better than BigBear.ai ›

Choosing between these specialized AI plays requires balancing high-stakes government work against commercial data engineering growth. Investors must decide if BigBear.ai (BBAI -8.67%) or Innodata (INOD -8.91%) better suits their portfolio needs.

BigBear.ai provides complex decision intelligence tools for federal and defense agencies, while Innodata helps major tech firms train generative AI systems. Both companies operate in the rapidly evolving world of artificial intelligence but serve different markets. This comparison examines their financial health, customer concentration, and valuation to help you decide which stock fits your strategy.

BigBear.ai offers decision intelligence solutions that combine data from different sources to help leaders make faster choices. It serves critical sectors like national security, cybersecurity, and global supply chains. According to its latest annual report, a specific contract accounted for nearly 51% of consolidated revenue. Customer concentration like this adds a layer of risk to the business.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached nearly $142.1 million. This represented a revenue growth decline of approximately 1.5% compared to the prior period. The company reported a net loss of roughly $180.2 million during this same time frame.

Following its early 2026 debt conversion, the debt-to-equity ratio is approximately 0.0x, meaning the company carries virtually no debt relative to its equity. The current ratio, which measures the ability to pay short-term debts with short-term assets, is close to 1.8x. In the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow was negative at nearly $46.3 million.

Innodata operates as a global data engineering firm that prepares and evaluates data for large-scale AI systems. The company serves several high-profile technology leaders, with one customer in its digital data solutions segment accounting for nearly 58% of total revenue in 2025. This focus on the best small cap tech stocks in the data sector highlights its role in the generative AI boom.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, 2026, revenue reached approximately $350.2 million. This reflects a revenue growth increase of nearly 46.9% over the previous year. The company also achieved a net income of roughly $48.5 million during this period.

As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the current ratio is approximately 2.7x. The debt-to-equity ratio is 0.0x, indicating a strong balance sheet with no total debt. In the fiscal year ended Dec. 31, 2025, free cash flow was nearly $35.6 million. Note that stock-based compensation represented roughly 23.8% of operating cash flow, which inflates reported cash generation since SBC is a non-cash expense added back in the cash flow statement.

BigBear.ai faces significant risk due to heavy revenue concentration with government customers who can terminate contracts for convenience. Scaling its sales efforts remains a challenge while managing long and unpredictable sales cycles. It also faces intense competition from Microsoft (MSFT -2.77%) and Amazon (AMZN -4.10%). The company also navigates complex AI deployment risks and recent financial volatility including litigation over accounting errors.

Innodata relies heavily on a single customer that contributed nearly 58% of its 2025 revenues, making it vulnerable to contract changes. It competes against well-resourced firms like Accenture (ACN +1.19%) and Cognizant Technology Solutions (CTSH +3.11%) in the data engineering space. The business also deals with geopolitical risks from its global workforce and ongoing legal challenges regarding securities class actions.

Innodata appears to offer a more attractive value proposition based on its lower P/S ratio, which measures market value against sales over the past twelve months.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

I'd go with Innodata. AI models are only as good as the data they learn from, and Innodata helps big tech companies create and test that data. Its sales have grown year over year for 13 straight quarters, and it makes money.

Last quarter, sales jumped 53% and management raised its growth outlook, though the stock has fallen sharply this fall. There are risks: Two customers make up most of its sales, margins narrowed, and one program is winding down. But Innodata is adding new customers and work from a large cloud company, and expects to replace the lost program by early next year.

To give BigBear.ai its due, it does high-stakes work for national security and border security customers with contracts that are difficult to replace, and its backlog is growing. But it's still losing money, and government contract sales can be difficult to predict. That uncertainty is where Innodata stands apart: It is already profitable, with plenty of cash to keep investing as demand for AI data grows.

#3074. Here's the Average Social Security Benefit at Age 67

Full retirement age is the baseline for your check, but where most 67-year-olds actually land may surprise you.

By Kailey Hagen, CFP® – Nov 20, 2026 at 6:00PM EST

The average Social Security benefit for 67-year-olds is $2,016.48 per month, according to Social Security Administration (SSA) data from December 2025. Full retirement age (FRA) ranges from 66 to 67, depending on your birth year, and it's 67 for everyone born in 1960 or later. It's the point where you're eligible for your unreduced retirement amount.

Reaching this age doesn't always mean you receive exactly 100% of your baseline amount. Many people who are 67 today have a full retirement age of 66 years and 10 months. These workers may have already earned small delayed retirement credits if they waited until 67 to claim their checks.

Earnings histories also impact these averages. Men who are 67 receive an average of $2,234.41 per month, while the average among women is $1,801.82. This results in a monthly gender gap of $432.59 as of December 2025.

Think of your full retirement age as the baseline for your benefits. The SSA bases your check on your average monthly income over your 35 highest-earning years, adjusting those wages for inflation and applying the primary insurance amount formula. This calculation creates the standard amount you're entitled to at full retirement age.

You don't have to claim at your baseline. You can earn delayed retirement credits, which are increases for delaying past FRA, worth 8% per year up to age 70. For a worker whose FRA is 67, waiting those three extra years provides a 24% total increase in monthly income.

These decisions have a visible impact on the data. The average monthly benefit at age 70 is $2,274.68, which is $258.20 higher than the age-67 average.

But even for these claimants, benefits alone often aren't enough to live on. Social Security was only designed to replace about 40% of pre-retirement wages, so most retirees rely on savings or other income to cover their full costs.

A 3.6% cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) will take effect in 2027 to help benefits keep pace with inflation. This will add about $73 a month on top of the average listed above. These adjustments are designed to protect your purchasing power over time.

Knowing the population average gives you a target for your own planning, but your final check depends on your career earnings and when you choose to claim.

Waiting until age 67 to claim Social Security helps you avoid the early claiming benefit reduction, which can drastically reduce your checks. This is one major reason FRA is such a popular claiming age, but it's not the only one.

When you claim Social Security under your FRA, you're also at risk of losing some of your benefits to the earnings test if you're still working. In 2027, for example, you lose $1 from your checks for every $2 you earn over $25,960 if you're under your FRA all year. If you'll reach your FRA next year, you only lose $1 for every $3 you earn over $67,920.

These lost benefits come back to you as a permanent benefit boost at your FRA. But for some, the risk of short-term losses makes them delay their claim until their FRA, when the earnings test no longer applies. This is something to weigh if you plan to continue working once you're eligible for Social Security checks.

For more on how Social Security affects your retirement, see this guide.

#3072. PayPal vs. SoFi Technologies: Which Financial Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

PayPal's global reach and SoFi's shift to profitability pull in opposite directions, and the valuation gap between them may tip the debate for 2027 investors.

By Sara Appino – Updated Nov 20, 2026 at 6:05PM EST

Deciding between PayPal (PYPL -5.46%) and SoFi Technologies (SOFI -7.76%) requires balancing established market dominance against aggressive digital growth. Both companies are fighting for a larger share of your digital wallet today.

PayPal remains a global giant in payment processing, while SoFi is a fast-growing digital bank targeting a younger, high-earning demographic. While they operate in different niches, both compete for the same pool of consumer financial activity, making them frequent candidates for a fintech-focused portfolio as investors look toward the future of money.

PayPal operates a massive digital payments platform serving nearly 439 million active accounts across 200 markets. It provides branded checkout services, person-to-person payments via Venmo, and even Bitcoin (CRYPTO:BTC) trading within its app. This scale makes it one of the most recognizable names in fintech stocks today as it adapts to a changing financial landscape.

Reliable recent quarterly figures for at least one of these companies aren't available, so this comparison uses each company's latest full fiscal year. For FY 2025, PayPal reported revenue of nearly $33.2 billion, representing growth of approximately 4.3% over the prior year. The company generated net income of close to $5.2 billion, showing a steady ability to convert its high volume of transactions into profit for its shareholders.

As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the debt-to-equity ratio reached roughly 0.5x, comparing total debt to shareholder equity. The current ratio, which compares short-term assets to short-term liabilities, was approximately 1.3x. Free cash flow, or the cash remaining after paying for operations and equipment, was nearly $5.6 billion in FY 2025.

SoFi Technologies is a mobile-first financial services provider that offers a wide range of products including student loan refinancing, home loans, and investment tools. It serves approximately 15.8 million members and has expanded into business services through a partnership with Mastercard (MA -0.14%). The company also supports Bitcoin trading and provides modern banking infrastructure to other firms through its proprietary technology segments.

For its latest annual report covering FY 2025, SoFi Technologies generated revenue of nearly $3.6 billion, which was an increase of approximately 35.1% compared to the previous year. The company reported net income of close to $481.3 million, marking a significant transition into consistent profitability. This growth was driven by a diversified mix of lending products and financial services that attracted new members to its platform.

Based on its December 2025 balance sheet, the debt-to-equity ratio was roughly 0.2x, helping investors see the low level of debt relative to shareholder equity. The current ratio was approximately 0.2x, which measures a company's ability to cover immediate liabilities with assets that can be quickly converted to cash. Free cash flow for the year was a net loss of nearly $4.0 billion, which often occurs in banking as capital is deployed into new loans rather than held as cash.

PayPal faces intense competition from both traditional banks and newer technology platforms that offer digital checkout solutions. The company is also subject to extensive global regulatory oversight regarding payment processing and anti-money laundering rules. Any failure to manage cybersecurity risks or protect user data could lead to significant penalties and damage its reputation.

SoFi Technologies must manage the complex regulatory requirements that come with being a bank holding company. It relies on a concentrated number of whole loan purchasers, meaning its results could suffer if these buyers reduce their activity. Additionally, the company faces the challenge of managing rapid growth across its various lending and technology segments without increasing credit risks.

When comparing these two companies using Forward P/E, PayPal looks significantly cheaper than its peer based on future earnings estimates.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

I'd go with SoFi Technologies. Its sales and earnings are both growing far faster than PayPal's, and it has built a full digital bank with more than 17 million members, starting from student loans. Its bank charter lets it fund loans with its members' deposits, a cost advantage most fintech rivals lack.

SoFi also gave investors its 2027 outlook a full quarter early, and it came in above analysts' expectations, with revenue expected to grow about 27%. Management built it assuming interest rates keep rising, even after a rate hike this year hurt the stock. The shares are still down for the year, so long-term investors get a lower price for a profitable, growing business.

To give PayPal its due, it is a household name that produces plenty of cash, and Venmo is growing fast. But its original business, letting shoppers pay online with their PayPal account, is barely growing, and overall sales are rising slowly. By contrast, SoFi's lending and banking businesses are surging, and even its technology business, which lost a large client, is expected to return to growth in 2027. For long-term investors, that mix of growth, profits, and a lower price makes SoFi the better buy for 2027.

#3071. Prediction: Rocket Lab Will Triple Its Revenue by 2028. Here’s What That Would Mean for the Stock.

What sounds outlandish on the surface actually makes a lot of sense once you dive deeper into the details of this space-launch company’s efforts.

By James Brumley – Nov 20, 2026 at 6:30PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Rocket Lab ›

Plenty of companies are growing incredibly well right now, including several from the artificial intelligence business. A company that's set to triple its top line over the course of the next couple of years, though? That seems outrageous.

Except for orbital launch and satellite tech specialist Rocket Lab (RKLB -10.92%), it isn't. This future business is already lined up. These paying customers are just waiting for one pivotal milestone that's likely to materialize sometime next year. Then the floodgates open.

Rocket Lab offers satellite launch solutions. Its relatively small Electron rocket design has now been launched 100 times, putting 273 satellites into low earth orbit.

The organization also provides a range of satellite components and construction services, which accounts for more than half of last quarter's revenue of $268 million. Rocket Lab expects to do around $980 billion in business in fiscal 2026, well up from last year's $602 billion.

This is just a taste of what's to come, though. As the company announced with its third-quarter report posted earlier this month, Rocket Lab's backlog grew 52% from Q2's $2.36 billion to an incredible $3.6 billion. The organization just needs more capacity, but that's coming. Rocket Lab can now manufacture an Electron rocket every eight days.

Then there's the proverbial 800-pound gorilla in the room. That's the company's larger Neutron rocket, which has seen its inaugural flight pushed back several times, with the first flight now expected sometime in 2027.

Despite the delays so far, once Neutron is flying, everything takes a turn for the better. As CFO Adam Spice commented during the third-quarter's earnings conference call, adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) "will turn positive within six months of that first successful test flight," with cash flow likely to turn positive shortly after that.

Neutron isn't the big profit center in and of itself, to be clear. It's just a means to an end. Being able to put larger, heavier satellites into orbit also opens up its satellite tech business. Of its new backlog of $3.6 billion, $1.7 billion of it reflects space systems rather than launch services, up 194% year over year.

To this end, while analysts aren't yet looking for Rocket Lab's revenue to triple between now and 2028, with recent failures of similar rockets like ULA's Vulcan and one earlier in the year from Blue Origin, Rocket Lab is quickly becoming a preferred launch option. It may well exceed 2028's expectations.

As for the impact on the stock, it's not a complicated prediction to make. If the company's top line comes anywhere close to tripling by 2028, the bullish argument firms up enormously.

While it may not triple in value in step with revenue, $3 billion in sales could turn this high-growth $34 billion outfit into a $60 billion outfit with relative ease. And given that the company isn't likely to raise money by issuing more stock anytime soon, that translates into a near-doubling of the stock's price.

You're getting some help in the meantime, by the way. The analyst community overwhelmingly considers RKLB stock a strong buy right now, with a 12-month consensus price target of $93.30 already more than 80% above the ticker's current price.

#3070. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#3068. Highlights: Area 51 helps San Antonio bounce back with a win over the Clippers

The San Antonio Spurs needed to bounce back after a disappointing loss to the Detroit Pistons on Tuesday night. A back-and-forth contest with the struggling Los Angeles Clippers wasn't exactly the dominant response some might have wanted, but the Spurs pulled out a 124-112 win.

The Spurs needed leading performances from their stars to outlast the Clippers. As they have so many times before, San Antonio relied on Area 51, the duo of Stephon Castle and Victor Wembanyama.

Castle stuffed the stat sheet with 21 points, 12 assists, 6 rebounds, and 4 steals (and 8 turnovers, but that's another story). He was slinging the ball all over the floor, including on this full-court pass that led to a transition "shunk" by Wembanyama.

Yes, you read that right. Shunk. A shot and a dunk.

Wembanyama told reporters after the game that he has a blister that has been hurting his hand on dunks. So when he had the chance, he dunked the ball without touching the rim. So the shunk was born.

Castle's ability to draw two defenders is one of his best skills. Here, he jumps Kris Dunn's pass to Brook Lopez and streaks to the other end, drawing two defenders and kicks it to Julian Champagnie for a wide-open three.

Defense to offense was the name of the game for both teams on Thursday. The Spurs scored 19 points in transition and 25 points off turnovers.

Castle didn't just get it done on the break. He had a balanced game in the half-court, orchestrating the offense for the Spurs with Dylan Harper sitting out. His connection with Wembanyama was on full display on this lob, as he once again drew the attention of multiple players, freeing up the big man for an easy lob.

Wembanyama got to the rim more often than he did against Detroit. The big man had 31 points and 11 rebounds, scoring 22 of those points inside the arc.

Wembanyama added 3 assists, including this sweet wraparound pass to Keldon Johnson. KJ was a key contributor off the bench, adding 10 points, 5 rebounds, 3 assists, and 2 steals.

Perhaps the best play of the night came from another bench player, Harrison Barnes. The veteran got out in transition and rose, using all 6 feet 8 inches of his frame to slam the ball home over the springy shot-blocking Yanic Konan Niederhauser. The "unc" has turned back the clock a few times this season. San Antonio will need him as they wait for Harper and Tobias Harris to heal up and rejoin the lineup.

The defense, particularly down the stretch, helped the Spurs get back into the game. Devin Vassell was a key part of their defensive effort, racking up 3 steals in the win. This is just an all-out effort play. Vassell gets a difficult strip on Darius Garland, grabs the 50/50 ball, and dunks it home in transition.

San Antonio will look to build on the win in a matchup against the New Orleans Pelicans on Saturday. To see full highlights from the Clippers game, check out the video below.

#3067. Prediction: Nvidia Stock Will Do This in the 6 Months After a New CEO Takes Over, According to History

Nvidia is searching for a new CEO following Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation.

By Adam Spatacco – Nov 20, 2026 at 6:42PM EST

Nvidia (NVDA -14.01%) is navigating one of the most consequential leadership transitions in recent Silicon Valley history. Following founder Jensen Huang's unexpected resignation amid a controversy involving alleged connections to Chinese intelligence, investors are growing increasingly concerned about the company's future. This uncertainty is weighing heavily on Nvidia stock, even as its underlying business continues to deliver extraordinary financial results.

Colette Kress, Nvidia's former chief financial officer, is now serving as interim CEO while the board searches for a permanent successor. According to reports, the company is considering external candidates, including Arm Holdings CEO Rene Haas.

History offers some clues about how technology stocks behave following major CEO transitions. However, Nvidia's circumstances are quite different from those of its peers, making the company's financial outlook particularly important right now.

Microsoft (MSFT -2.77%) represents a successful example of a technology leadership transition. Satya Nadella became CEO on Feb. 4, 2014, replacing Steve Ballmer after Microsoft conducted a formal succession search. Nadella had previously led Microsoft's cloud and enterprise businesses, giving him extensive knowledge of the company's operations.

Microsoft stock advanced roughly 19% during Nadella's first six months as CEO. Of course, over the long term, he helped transform the Windows developer into a cloud computing powerhouse.

Alphabet (GOOGL -2.70%) (GOOG -2.59%) offers another example. Sundar Pichai became CEO of Alphabet on Dec. 3, 2019, succeeding co-founder Larry Page. However, Pichai had already served as Google's CEO since 2015, making the transition more of a consolidation of leadership responsibilities than an unexpected management overhaul.

Alphabet stock was higher roughly six months later, despite experiencing a meaningful drawdown during the short-lived COVID-19 market crash. Moreover, Page and his co-founder Sergey Brin remained involved as shareholders and directors, providing continuity that Nvidia currently lacks.

Amazon (AMZN -4.10%) experienced a different outcome when Andy Jassy succeeded Jeff Bezos on July 5, 2021. Jassy was another internal executive who had previously built and led Amazon Web Services (AWS). Although Bezos relinquished the CEO position, he remains executive chairman, preserving his influence over the company's long-term strategy.

Amazon shares declined approximately 6% during Jassy's first six months as CEO. At the time, investors were assessing the impacts of slowing pandemic-era growth and rising operating expenses on Amazon's e-commerce marketplace.

The three examples above show that CEO succession can bring different short-term outcomes. However, they also share an important characteristic: Each of these companies promoted an experienced internal executive through an orderly transition, unlike Nvidia's abrupt leadership disruption.

Nvidia's situation is unusual because Huang wasn't just another corporate executive. He co-founded Nvidia in 1993 and spent more than three decades directing the company's evolution from a graphics chip manufacturer into the world's leading supplier of artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure. His influence extended across engineering, product development, corporate culture, and customer relationships.

As a result of his departure, investors are increasingly questioning whether Nvidia can maintain its technological edge without the executive who helped establish it. The uncertainty is compounded by growing competition from custom AI accelerators developed by Alphabet, Amazon, and other hyperscalers. Nvidia's next CEO needs to prove they can preserve the company's innovation-driven culture while navigating complicated supply chains and regulatory relationships.

Despite the unusual circumstances, Nvidia's financial outlook provides a compelling reason to anticipate a meaningful recovery. During its fiscal third-quarter earnings call, management increased its fiscal 2028 revenue growth forecast to 75%, up from its previous outlook of 70%. This suggests demand for Nvidia's accelerated computing platforms remains strong despite Huang's departure.

Furthermore, Nvidia expects to generate $130 billion in revenue during the fourth quarter -- representing 91% growth year over year. The company is also forecasting gross margins of between 72% and 73% in fiscal 2028, suggesting that accelerating sales growth will translate into additional profits.

Nvidia's share repurchase program provides another subtle catalyst for the stock. In September, the board increased the company's existing authorization by $150 billion, leaving approximately $227.3 billion available at the end of the third quarter. Management intends to execute the remaining authorization by the end of fiscal 2028.

By reducing the number of outstanding shares, stock buybacks can increase earnings per share (EPS) even if net income is unchanged. Combined with Nvidia's revenue growth and profit margins, upcoming share repurchases should fuel substantial EPS expansion over the coming year.

My prediction is that Nvidia stock will trade higher six months after its new CEO assumes the role. The appointment itself should remove the primary source of uncertainty around the company, allowing investors to concentrate once again on revenue growth, earnings power, and competitive advantages.

#3060. Neemias Queta is flourishing

"I feel like his growth and his role has gone kind of under the radar," Joe Mazzulla said of Neemias Queta after the Celtics' 131-94 win over the Mavericks on Sunday night.

Coming into last season, we did not know what to expect from Neemias Queta as he entered his first season as the Celtics starting center. He had some good moments in his first two years as a Celtic, but nothing to show he was going to be the long-term center.

There were growing pains at the start. The Celtics lost games early on due to their lack off rebounding but since that point, Queta has surpassed even his biggest fans' expectations.

With Queta as the closest defender this year, teams are shooing 41.3% on shots less than 6 feet away from the rim. That is the best mark in the NBA among players who have faced 4 or more shots around the rim per game. The average mark is 63%.

According to databallr, teams shoot 20% worse in general when Queta is on the court.

Databallr has a stat called DDPM which "estimates how much a player improves expected team defense per 100 possessions. It rolls together rim deterrence, contests, rebounding, disruption, foul discipline, and context-adjusted defensive information." Neemias Queta's DDPM is +2.3, which ranks in the 98th percentile of the NBA.

That shows in Queta's on/off numbers as well. With Queta on the floor this year, the Celtics are giving up 102.5 points per 100 possessions (compared to 112.1 with him on the bench) and have a net rating of +23.8 when he is on the floor (compared to +0.2 with him off of it).

Perhaps there is no better example of Neemy's impact than at the end of the Raptors game when he blocked 2 shots at the rim late in the game to seal a Celtics win. One of them came up one with 17 seconds left and the other with the Celtics up 5 with 4 seconds left.

One more block for Neemias Queta to end the game. Celtics pull out the win on the 2nd night of a back to back after being down as many as 22 points! https://t.co/YS3DB6PeDQ

— Celtics Clips (@celticsclipss) November 12, 2026

In what was a five-point win for the Celtics — Queta was a +27. His impact on both ends of the court cannot be overstated.

Queta is not a good defensive center; he is one of the best defenders in the NBA. The way he impacts the rim allows for the Celtics to play more aggressive on defense, which is something they wanted to do this season.

He is anchoring the 2nd best defense in the NBA right now. The Celtics defensive rating is 106.0, just 2nd in the NBA behind the Minnesota Timberwolves, at 104.0.

He has started to take and make corner threes this year. He doesn't shoot them a lot; he has only shot 6 to start the season but he has made 4 of them. He isn't taking contested threes or any threes above the break so we can't call him a three-point threat or a stretch big, but he has made the ones he takes so I can't complain about them.

The advanced numbers say his offense has been average. Databallr's ODPM has Queta at 0.0, in the 67th percentile and the Celtics only score 1.00 points per possession with Queta as the roll man in the pick and roll.

However, the on/off numbers say Boston is significantly better on offense with Queta on the court. The Celtics score 126.4 points with Queta on the floor and 112.1 with Queta off the floor. If we take Jayson Tatum off the court, the Celtics are scoring 131.4 points per 100 possessions with Queta on the court. Granted that is just 74 minutes, but it shows that these numbers are not skewed thanks to most of Queta's minutes happening with Tatum on the floor.

One reason for this is that he has the most screen assist points in the NBA per 36 minutes, with 15 points per 36 minutes. He has also been setting a ton of flare screens, which are off ball screens set and a player moves away from the ball. His screening creates a ton of opportunities for Boston in the half court.

The Celtics have gotten a lot of praise for their player development. They might not have a bigger win than Queta. Turning him into one of the best defensive centers in the NBA is not something many (including the Sacramento Kings who waived him) saw coming. Having Queta as a security blanket at the rim has been a big deal for the Celtics this year as their defense has become one of the best units in the NBA.

#3055. Open Thread: How the 24-second clock became the standard for the NBA.

As I've stated on many an occasion, I am a math geek. So you can imagine my pleasure in fiding this post:

The 24-second clock that is used in the NBA came about through a mathematical means.

Apparently before the adoption of the 24-second clock, teams simply dribbled out games for much longer than what qualifies as garbage time these days. One of the team owners came up with the equations that the game, which was forty-eight minutes, or 2280 seconds, divided by the average number of shots teams took in a game, or 120 shots, was equal to 24 seconds per shot. Thus eliciting a more timely and interesting game.

I have not personally verified the validity, but now I have plans during the holiday break.

Spurs took a win last night, nice to see when the moments meshed well. That said, Harper's absence was felt.

#3051. The Phoenix Suns’ core four are getting better, with more runway to grow

The Phoenix Suns are 7-7 overall after a 1-5 start to the season. We were hoping for a much quicker start to the season than last year, but the difficulty of opponents, combined with the addition of Miles Bridges and Oso Ighodaro's injury, meant the Suns were not at their best to start the season.

The Suns are now 6-2 since Ighodaro was inserted into the starting lineup. Devin Booker is back to playing at a near All-NBA level this season. Jordan Ott is learning the right rotations. Dillon Brooks looks like he is in full villain mode on both ends of the floor after an up-and-down week in November that culminated in a 1-point performance in a loss to the Lakers. His move in the rotation to be the go-to scorer with the second unit to begin the second and fourth quarters appears to have unlocked him.

Miles Bridges is starting to find his lanes and facilitate an offense that needs to keep moving. His defense and rebounding are getting better as well.

Even Jalen Green, who finds himself in trade chatter, in the last two games, has had inspiring second halves to show what Brian Gregory's vision of this team is supposed to look like. Green's 18-point, seven-assist and seven-rebound night was a glimpse into who the Suns need him to be more consistently as one of the best athletes and downhill threats on this team. Combine that with the energy he played with against the Nuggets in that second half, where he had multiple steals, and maybe, just maybe, this backcourt pairing might work between him and Booker. The fit will never be perfect, but if Green can play this way more consistently, it will quiet a lot of the noise around him for the time being.

Regardless of the level of opponent, the Suns offense was crisp and flowing all night long against the Kings. The ball movement, spacing, and unselfishness jumped off the television screen. All four of the Suns starters had over five assists against the Kings; Ighodaro only had two. But that level of effort and commitment to making each other better on both ends of the floor started against the Nuggets in a win Monday night.

In back-to-back games, the core four have all scored in double figures, and the only occurrence of someone scoring below 15 points was Bridges against the Kings; he scored 13 points. Against the Nuggets, the core four combined for 67 points, and against the Kings they combined for 80 points.

The numbers also back it up as well. Before the Suns played the Magic on November 5, the core four had a -59 over the first seven games of the season. Since then, it is only a -2 in eight games, and the group continues to play better. The eye test also shows that they are playing better as well.

This group still has a long way to go, and it still has yet to put it together against a high-quality opponent like the Thunder or Spurs, but the good news is the next seven games on the schedule are: at Utah Jazz, at New Orleans Pelicans, at Houston Rockets, home vs Dallas Mavericks, at Utah Jazz, home vs Los Angeles Clippers, and home vs Sacramento. The Suns should realistically finish this stretch 5-2 or better.

However, it is even deeper than the record over the next seven games. The Suns need to continue to prove that how they have played the last two games is who they are. They are still building winning habits and are still working through defensive and rebounding lapses that have plagued them all season.

Does the 3-point shooting efficiency continue for Booker? Can Green play with the defensive intensity and playmaking? Can Brooks sustain the energy to be the emotional leader night in and night out? Does Miles Bridges continue to do the little things like rebounding and defending regardless of his shot attempts? And as a group, can this core four show up and put teams away without sleepwalking through quarters that give the lesser teams life?

This team still has flaws that might be too difficult to overcome, but if the Suns answer yes to most of these questions over the next two weeks, then they will be right back up into the top eight in the Western Conference when the calendar turns to December.

#3050. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#3048. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#3046. Knicks 102, Timberwolves 99: “3 straight on the road, what’s up…”

LaMelo Ball hit one of his patented three-pointers from the left elbow early in the third quarter against the New York Knicks last night and as the crowd cheered, he proceeded to jog back on defense. Of course, the only not totally broken Ball brother couldn't let the moment pass without completely indulging himself into a thoroughly scripted "ice in the veins"-gesture that feels as original these days as a two-handed set shot. Just a moment later Josh Hart rained on the parade, running directly by Vanilla Ball's D and finishing an easy backdoor cut with a simple layup. And just like that the Knicks were up 14 again, ignoring the protocol of how cutting a deficit to 12 in a game where your team got handsomely dominated up to that point needs to be celebrated.

It feels good to root for a professional team, doesn't it? It will allow you to say things like Real Clydes Wear Plaid shared in the Postgame Thread and which I unapologetically stole for the headline of this recap. The Knicks won their third game in a row at Minnesota last night and they probably made it a lot harder than it needed to be. Their opponents certainly had something to do with it, no matter how ill-advised some individual emotions might have been. The Timberwolves can play tremendous defense, are as long as those trees in their logo and have an aggression you could certainly link to that name-giving animal of them. They got a good albeit cranky coach, a proven system, years of winning experience. Yet in the end they also got LaMelo Ball now.

We get that he is a talented, at times iconic passer; we also get that he has found his footing fairly well on the frozen tundra near the Great Lakes so far. But we really can't get convinced that you can ever really win big with a "talent" like that. His play can remind one of the game young kids play before they really get a hang of Dr. Naismith's rules. They also take two steps before every dribble, then four after they pick the ball up. They also couldn't care less about a repetitive shooting motion or dribbling the ball without palming it every time. With the kids it's okay and fun, we let them discover themselves as players and excuse it. But Lamborghini LaMelo should be held to a different standard, he is supposed to be a professional NBA player after all.

Luckily for the Knicks, they got plenty of real pros on their roster, guys who truly care about winning and treat their profession like it means something – not only to them but to others. It certainly seemed that way when they played most of the game last night with a level of calmness and concentration that has been a hallmark of theirs ever since they went on that beautiful championship run last year. But despite all their dominance over that two-month stretch in the playoffs, you can't ignore that they are a team with plenty of cracks in their armor, many of which showed themselves last night as well. It's the kind of weakness that are often the reason for the Bockers falling behind big only to have the opportunity to stage their beloved monumental comebacks later on.

Yesterday the Knicks offense completely bogged down late when Minnesota went to more switching on D (raise your hand if you've seen that before), neutralizing all the beautiful team basketball that helped the Gotham Gang run up an almost 20-point lead earlier. Not even Karl-Anthony Towns, once again mesmerizing for most of the night against a traditional five at his old stomping grounds, couldn't regain his first-half form. Maybe he rode the pine a little long, Mr. Brown? Well, you can't play him all 48, we get it. A lineup that included Jose Alvarado, Andre Drummond and Josh Hart was still an interesting choice, to say the least. For all the individual chops it's quite a load for Jalen Brunson to carry that group offensively, it almost feels like him heaving big AD up K2 or some other majestic mountain. It might get you to the summit against certain defenses but considering some defenses are as long and physical as the Wolf Pack, it can also get cold rather quickly. Especially when Brunson still hasn't found his touch inside the paint yet.

That New York could still pull it out in the end is a testament to timely plays and a gritty, opportunistic defense that at no point in the game relented to the physically imposing Wolves coming at them. When LaMelo Ball couldn't find someone open downtown, the canines' only plan was to ram their oversized combo forwards ahead. It worked here or there but never consistently. Watch out, there's an extended arm coming at you – boom, another offensive foul! It's an interesting yet understandable choice by the league to make the off arm a point of emphasis this year (sorry, SGA). Why didn't they do the same for palming the basketball or taking more steps than a dressage horse? Your guess is as good as mine.

For the moment, though, let us not worry about it – there is a win to be celebrated. For all we know though, our guys are probably watching some film right now and preparing for their next opponent (Portland on Saturday). Maybe they are also speeding race cars through some busy pedestrian zone or practice their one-legged step-back fadeaway jumper that has netted them less than 42 percent shooting for their career?

Nah, we leave that to the guy with ice in his veins.

→ Baca artikel asli

#3040. Bucks vs. Trail Blazers Player Grades: Ament drops career high, Rollins returns with a vengeance

On yet another night when the Milwaukee Bucks matched up against a high-profile former player (or two), they couldn't get it done, losing to the Portland Trail Blazers 124-114. The Blazers gave the Bucks chances early, looking like they were the ones on the second night of a back-to-back, not the Bucks. Unfortunately, the Bucks failed to capitalise on those chances and fell at the mercy of two former star Milwaukee point guards, Damian Lillard and Jrue Holiday, who taught the young Bucks a lesson down the stretch. Read our full summary of the game here and catch a six-minute audio recap on the Bucks+ podcast, Bucks In Six Minutes, below.

31 minutes, 19 points, 4 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 4 turnovers, 7/13 FG, 4/7 3P, 1/3 FT, -16

Herro's counting stats look fine, but it felt like he played a lot worse. Herro started the game on autopilot, and his turnovers all felt like pick-sixes, especially the one in the fourth quarter where he got ripped trying to bring the ball up against Holiday. I generally don't lean too much into single-game plus/minus, but this one feels dead on. All things being equal—which they may not have been—Herro should not have closed the game.

21 minutes, 15 points, 3 rebounds, 6 assists, 4 steals, 3 turnovers, 5/11 FG, 1/5 3P, 4/6 FT, +5

You can basically take everything I said about Herro and flip it for Rollins. His impact on the game was monumental, and most of it came on defence. Even more impressive, he did this while coming off an illness. Rollins deserved more minutes than he got (assuming he wasn't on a minutes restriction).

22 minutes, 9 points, 1 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 steal, 1 turnover, 3/11 FG, 1/4 3P, -5

Jaquez was bad. He contributed virtually nothing on offence, and with Avdija out, there wasn't really a defensive matchup for him. I especially hate the possessions where Jaquez dribbles aimlessly without gaining any advantage on his defender; it feels like they always end in a turnover.

25 minutes, 11 points, 5 rebounds, 2 steals, 4/8 FG, 2/4 3P, -5

Ware was… decent? Like, the three-ball is coming back, and he continues to show stuff defensively, but you know what I loved? That monster offensive rebound and putback he had in the fourth quarter. That showed a level of emotion and care factor we have rarely seen from Ware. Inject it into my veins!

28 minutes, 14 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 2 steals, 3 turnovers, 5/13 FG, 2/5 3P, -11

A bit of a rookie game for Burries, with a bunch of good stuff and some not-so-good stuff. He's got that floater going left on lock, so that's good. His defence felt really impactful, and the play where he prevented Scoot Henderson from even crossing half court was stupendous. I will say that Burries' release is going to have to speed up in the future; guys are getting to his shots starting from far away. All in all, I thought the rook played better than what his stat line would suggest.

22 minutes, 18 points, 3 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 steal, 2 turnovers, 6/11 FG, 3/6 3P, 3/5 FT, -6

A third really solid game in a row from Jakucionis, who should have closed over Herro. At this point, it's basically become a theme that he comes into the game, makes stuff happen, and, most importantly, brings energy. Now, some silly stuff is going to come with that, but you take the good with the bad, because there is undoubtedly more good. Finally, love that he's not even hesitating on his three-ball right now.

35 minutes, 18 points, 9 rebounds, 0 turnovers, 6/11 FG, 2/5 3P, 4/4 FT, -7

On balance, this was Ament's best game of his young NBA career, and it showed in the number of minutes he played. I truly think his shot has a high chance of going in every time he shoots, and that's a great sign for the future. I also love that Ament is attacking rebounds more and more, especially on the offensive glass; his game is becoming more multifaceted with each passing week. Plus, no turnovers! My only negative—and it is a big one—is that his off-ball defence remains a wreck; overhelping was a major issue last night.

27 minutes, 7 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 block, 0 turnovers, 3/8 FG, 1/5 3P, +8

I know it's become a bit of a cliché to say that "good things happen when Nance is on the floor," but it's true! His line isn't impressive, but the offence just flows when the two-way big is on the court in a way it doesn't necessarily with other players. Just wait until that three-ball starts dropping. More please!

11 minutes, 3 points, 3 rebounds, 1/2 FG, 0

I considered leaving LeVert off, but I couldn't ignore his impact as a rebounder in the fourth. He obviously had that ridiculous and-one over Clingan, but LeVert grabbed like three O-boards in one possession; unfortunately, the Bucks couldn't pay it off with a bucket.

I've already alluded to Jenkins making what I believed was the wrong call closing with Herro over Jakucionis, but he also brought back Rollins—who was making a wildly positive impact—too late. Then there are the tough decisions that aren't entirely based on the now, like closing with Ament. You had to close with the rookie given the game he had played, but Lillard and Holiday kinda feasted on him late. That's the push-and-pull of coaching a rebuilding side. On the other hand, I loved the move to start Nance.

DNP-CD: AJ Green, Kam Jones, Bogoljub Markovic, Cormac Ryan

Inactive: Myles Turner, Kyle Kuzma, Ousmane Dieng

The veterans are not necessarily needed for this team to compete.

I found it ironic that Dame complained about a foul call where Ament hooked his arm and then drew a shooting foul. That's Dame's signature move! LOL.

Jrue was coaching up Brayden after the game. What a guy.

Speaking of sneaky foul-drawing, Jakucionis has the "drive hard at the hip, throw it up, and pray to the gods they call a foul" move on lock. What worries me is that, like it or not, those calls are generally reserved for guys with stature. Therefore, there will be—and have been—times when KJ does that, it doesn't get called, and he throws up a shot that is basically a turnover.

Gary Trent Jr.'s stint was absolutely diabolical. Exactly why I don't necessarily buy the idea that high steal numbers are a sign of a good defender.

Former Wisconsin Badger John Tonje was in the Blazers rotation.

Nate Ament spoke glowingly of Jrue Holiday in his postgame presser, highlighting his two-way impact.

The Bucks are back in action on Saturday afternoon against the Philadelphia 76ers in Philly. Tip-off is at 12:00 p.m. CST—watch on MY24.

#3035. Victor Wembanyama has turned his NBA finals humiliation into a revenge tour for the ages

It wouldn't be exactly right to say Victor Wembanyama didn't miss a beat after a disastrous NBA finals in June that left him and his San Antonio Spurs defeated, memed and embarrassed. In the Spurs' first game this season, against an Oklahoma City Thunder team since decimated by injuries, Wemby went an appalling 1-for-13 from the field. Ten of his 12 points came via free throws. After getting blocked by Aday Mara, he leaned over his knees and muttered furiously to himself as if trying to exorcise an intrusive thought. It brought to mind an image from the NBA finals a few months earlier: Wemby on the floor after being fouled by the Knicks' Mitchell Robinson, crowing, "I'm in your head!" The Spurs went on to blow a 29-point lead, lose the game and the finals. Now Wemby seemed too preoccupied with the issues within his own dome to worry about wreaking havoc in anybody else's.

Related: Steph Curry's defense of Ayesha exposes the ugly double standards of sports' manosphere | Andrew Lawrence

Game two saw Wembanyama elect to lay the ball up instead of dunking it in the dying seconds of a close tilt with the Houston Rockets, which the springy Amen Thompson blocked spectacularly. Not a great start, and in a league whose narratives calcify long before sample sizes reach an adequate scale, it allowed for whispers that the Spurs were in trouble. But you could say Wembanyama has not missed a beat since then. After beginning the season 0-2, the Spurs are now 13-3, with 12 of those wins coming in a row before a loss to the Detroit Pistons on Tuesday.

San Antonio's basketball hasn't been as seamless as those numbers suggest – the starting lineup includes three guards, leaving the offense a bit clunky and wanting for three-point shooting. It also includes Wembanyama, which papers over pretty much every possible problem.

Unofficially crowned the best player in the world during last season's playoffs, Wembanyama has since distanced his closest challengers for that honor. Somebody has stolen Nikola Jokić's three-point shot and hidden it in a deep hole, signaling what may prove to be the start of his decline after six consecutive imperious, MVP-caliber seasons. Meanwhile, Shai Gilgeous-Alexander's efficiency has inevitably suffered as he's tasked with keeping the injury-ridden Thunder afloat.

Wembanyama, meanwhile, has got better, averaging 28.6 points, 11.9 rebounds and countless plays in which his mere presence causes the opposing offense to spontaneously combust through the first 16 games of the season. The Lakers' Luka Dončić is playing the best basketball of his career this season, averaging gaudy offensive numbers that, if sustained, would represent the most dominant scoring season in modern NBA history. That Dončić is not the runaway early favorite for MVP is solely because of Wembanyama's two-way excellence.

Wembanyama's unprecedented impact on the game of basketball is already well-documented, though the man is only 22. He is at once a void and a force field on defense, vacuuming up plenty of shots with his inflatable-man arms and deterring others simply by standing in the paint. Before the Pistons beat the Spurs on Tuesday, their players warmed up by shooting over teammates who held long foam blocks over their heads, practicing shots with sufficiently steep arcs to escape Wemby's treacherous fingertips, trying to learn how to play the game on a vertical axis.

Wembanyama racked up plenty of offensive highlights during last year's playoffs – a half-court buzzer-beater, a logo three-pointer to tie a vital game in the final minute when the rest of the team had gone cold – but his defensive foundation and paint presence are so strong that any three-pointers he can generate are a bonus.

Wembanyama was maligned during the finals for the lack of a "go-to move", which clogged up the Spurs' offense as the Knicks erased double-digit deficits late in games as if they were playing a G League team. The Spurs' game against the Pistons offered a clue to what he's developing: twice in the first half, Wembanyama threw the ball off the backboard, to himself, so he could collect it and dunk as everybody else watched from the floor. A player this physically anomalous and versatile will eventually have a bag so deep nobody will be able to see the bottom.

Despite Wembanyama's visually obvious gifts, there are still times when the magnitude of their manifestation on a game is shocking. In a blowout against the Sacramento Kings, he needed only 25 minutes to rack up 40 points, 11 rebounds and eight blocks. In a close game against the Dallas Mavericks, he produced 52 points, 24 rebounds and eight blocks in 34 minutes. On one possession, he blocked three consecutive Mavericks shots, an entire defense unto himself. The Spurs won the game by one point; during Wembanyama's minutes, they outscored the Mavs by 23. "I haven't been feeling great lately," Wembanyama said after the game. "I'm sick. I felt like I got my legs back. So, it should be more like this more often." Well then.

Even what felt like a fairly quiet Wembanyama performance against the Los Angeles Clippers on Thursday night (he only recorded one block, just before the end of the third quarter) dictated the shape of the game. The numbers are one thing – 32 points, 11 rebounds, no biggie – and watching him is another. He threw down fierce alley-oop dunks. He galloped through the paint, an Ent trampling helpless orcs, for easy floaters. Late in the fourth quarter, he juggled the ball to himself above the basket, reminiscent of a child batting a balloon across a room at a birthday party, before tapping it into the basket.

He found himself with so much space on one play that he rose as if to dunk the ball and dropped it gently through the net instead, flicking his wrist as if he'd just shot a three. On one of those jammed-up Spurs offensive possessions, the Clippers' Isaiah Jackson was guarding Wembanyama tightly as the shot clock wound down. Wemby took a long two-pointer with Jackson's hand in his face, a bizarre shot created from thin air, and drained it.

Wembanyama's highlights won't get old until 7ft 4in generational talents become commonplace, but equally impressive at this point in his career is his maturity. The finals last season were a nightmare on a few fronts: he enabled a legion of detractors with overly violent fouls and dubious flops, then validated their harshest critiques by underperforming in vital games. It'd be enough to ruin plenty of mortals. Wembanyama has emerged from the adversity intact and evolved, keeping the Spurs firmly on track for a shot at redemption.

#3031. 49ers final injury report: Mike Evans is ‘going in the right direction’

San Francisco 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan spoke about the final injury report on Friday. The team did not rule out any of the 53-man roster. There are nine players with questionable designations:

WR Mike Evans (Achilles)LB Dre Greenlaw (neck)CB Upton Stout (shoulder)RB Jordan James (calf)DT CJ West (knee)CB Nate Hobbs (adductor)DE Khalid Kareem (ankle)QB Mac Jones (illness)LB Luke Gifford (chest)

When asked about his first day of practice on Thursday, Shanahan said, "He had a good day of practice yesterday. As always, he's a little sore today, but going in the right direction."

Shanahan said the team will see how Evans does during Saturday's walkthrough before deciding his game status. When asked how much of a difference Evans makes, Shanahan said: "I think the guys have done a good job of holding it down without Mike out there. Mike's our guy. A very unique player. Been doing it at a high level for a very long time. Played really well in the games he played this year. Be great to get him back."

The 49ers practiced at half speed on Friday, according to Shanahan. If that's the case, and knowing that Saturday is a walkthrough, it'd be a surprise if Evans wasn't in uniform this Sunday night.

Earlier in the month, Shanahan said they felt Evans could have played if the circumstances were different. After disposing of the Raiders and Cowboys, the next month's schedule will feature four consecutive teams jockeying for playoff position.

On Dre Greenlaw, Shanahan said, "Hoping he'll be alright. You never know with nerves and neck and stuff. But hoping it continues to go in the right direction."

Starting cornerbacks Renardo Green and Deommodore Lenoir are doing "a lot better" and are off the injury report. "Good. They didn't practice last week and weren't sure if they were going to fully play, and they're not on the injury report this week, so a lot better."

We've seen the other team's biggest pass-catching threat have a field day against the secondary in two straight games. Justin Jefferson is probably licking his chops after what he's seen in the past couple of games.

The 49ers need to decide whether cornerback Nate Hobbs or defensive tackle CJ West can improve their roster. Their 21-day practice window closes on December 2. If the Niners don't activate either player by Wednesday, the season is over for Hobbs and West.

The Hobbs signing was always strange, because he's never been the type of player who played on special teams. He didn't hold up on the perimeter at cornerback, so he became a slot by necessity. He's a veteran, but it's unclear where he'd help the secondary.

On Mac Jones feeling better: "I believe so. I think he's trying to get out there today."

#3028. Victor Wembanyama, NBA's dunk king, turns to the 'shunk' due to painful blister on hand

Victor Wembanyama has found a new way to shoot in order to give his hands a rest and prevent future blisters.

It's called the "shunk," as San Antonio Spurs color commentator Sean Elliott put it. It's basically the 7-foot-4 Wembanyama "dunking" the ball through the basket like a jump shot.

Wembanyama showed off the "shunk" during the Spurs' 124-112 win over the Los Angeles Clippers on Thursday. The two-time All-Star posted 32 points, 11 rebounds and 1 "shunk."

"I'm not going to lie," Wembanyama said afterward via ESPN. "Actually, I've had this blister forever, and it's killing me. And every time I dunk, it kills me. I've been dragging this blister for I don't know how long because of dunking. So, I did not [quit dunking], but if I can avoid it, I would."

Through 16 games, Wembanyama leads all players with 72 dunks — Thursday's "shunk" was counted as a dunk by the NBA. The next closest is Giannis Antetokounmpo of the Miami Heat, who has 49. The Spurs star is on pace for 369 dunks this season, which would pass Rudy Gobert's record of 306 he amassed during the 2018-19 season.

Was the "shunk" the shortest jump shot Wembanyama has ever attempted?

#3023. Why Sunday night’s game vs. the Vikings could be the most important one of the season

Sitting in first place in the NFC, the San Francisco 49ers look to be facing their toughest battle yet in Week 11, as they take on the Minnesota Vikings in primetime in Mexico City.

San Francisco had an outstanding offensive performance last weekend against the Dallas Cowboys, out-dueling their counterparts 46-40 to improve to 8-1, tied for the best record in the NFL. However, their ailing defense struggled in a big way against one of the more talented offenses in the league.

Now, they get a completely different challenge on Sunday night, taking on the league's best defense and best defensive coordinator with the highest stakes on the line: the NFC's No. 1 seed.

The 49ers are in the midst of their toughest stretch of the year. They just played the Cowboys, get the Vikings this week, and then get the Seattle Seahawks, New York Giants, and Los Angeles Rams, all potential playoff contenders, in the weeks after.

So, keeping their lead for the No. 1 seed is paramount, which means a win on Sunday is a top priority. Now normally, I'd vote for divisional games to be the most important ones for the 49ers, as nothing tops taking care of business in the tough NFC West.

But Sunday's game against the Vikings could actually be the most important one of the season for the 49ers, as there are serious implications with the No. 1 seed on the line.

If the 49ers win on Sunday, ESPN's Aaron Schatz, the creator of DVOA, projects San Francisco to have an 81 percent chance of getting the No. 1 seed in the NFC. Should they lose? That number falls all the way to 26 percent in Schatz's simulations, with the Vikings jumping from five percent to 51 percent for the No. 1 seed.

Now, the 49ers are still the team with the second-highest odds to win the Super Bowl on FanDuel at +550, behind the Buffalo Bills at +500. They also currently have the best odds to win the NFC (+250), with the Vikings (+470) second. We'll see how major that change is pending this weekend's outcome.

With the current state of the NFC West and how strong the 49ers have started the season, making the playoffs doesn't seem to be in jeopardy. The 49ers have -700 odds to win the NFC West, and FanDuel gives them -110 odds to win over 13.5 games this season.

But having the No. 1 seed would be huge for January, as the playoffs would run through San Francisco, giving them the best chance at making the Super Bowl in a very wide-open race this season.

San Francisco does get a strong end to its schedule. Three of their last four will be against the Los Angeles Chargers, Philadelphia Eagles, and Arizona Cardinals. But they have to weather the storm in the coming weeks with a couple of matchups against playoff hopefuls, starting with Sunday night against the Vikings.

Minnesota looked rejuvenated last weekend, beating the Green Bay Packers 30-10 on the road in Kirk Cousins's first start back. The 49ers just saw Cousins two weeks ago, and they'll have their hands full on both sides of the ball.

Heading into the game, San Francisco is a 3-point favorite over Minnesota, and they've got a ton at stake in the heavyweight NFC battle.

#3016. The one matchup that will tip 49ers-Vikings: Dallas Turner versus Trent Williams

Sunday night is a huge game for the San Francisco 49ers and the Minnesota Vikings. The winner will likely be in the driver's seat for the number one seed in the NFC with a tiebreaker over the other team. Now, there is no way to predict what will happen down the stretch, but this game feels like a playoff game and, for many, not all, the marbles.

The must-see matchup is clearly Kyle Shanahan against Brian Flores. Interesting subplots include Kirk Cousins facing the 49ers again and Jauan Jennings returning to face his old team. One matchup deserves a closer look and could tip the scales for both teams.

EDGE rusher Dallas Turner is in the mix for DPOY and rightfully so. However, Will Anderson IV might have a stranglehold after last night's performance. Everyone is all over Flores' blitz rates and the exotic nature of his coverages, but Turner has completely broken out in 2026.

The 23-year-old has a bright future ahead of him in the NFL. On the other side of the age spectrum, there stands 38-year-old Trent Williams. Sunday will be the first time the two will do battle.

Turner lines up everywhere, which is to be expected in Flores' scheme. The right side is where he has his highest snap numbers, with 253 snaps between RE and REO. Colton McKivitz will draw Turner also, but Williams will likely see Turner more.

According to PFF, Turner has generated 45 pressures, which is the sixth-most among qualified EDGE rushers. On 305 pass-blocking snaps in 2026, Williams has allowed one sack, two QB hits, five hurries, and eight pressures. Against two of the more ferocious pass rushes in Seattle and Denver, Williams posted back-to-back clean sheets.

Communication for the offensive line will be paramount on Sunday night as Flores will send the house more often than not, and many Vikings defensive linemen and rushers benefit from the confusion and chaos caused by Flores, but Turner vs. Williams is a matchup the 49ers have to win.

Sunday will be filled with plenty of star power, and Turner will have his impact, but against Williams, he must be turned away and shut out. This is the classic case of "old guard" vs. "new guard."

Regardless of your fandom, Sunday night is the best matchup of the weekend and possibly the season so far. Grab your popcorn, and watching two of the best in the business will be a delight for all.
